**2. Semantic comparison**  

Original loop body for `i` in `0..LEN_1D-2`:  
```
a[i] = b[i] + c[i] * d[i];
b[i] = c[i] + b[i];
a[i+1] = b[i] + a[i+1] * d[i];
```

Let’s trace dependencies:  

- Statement 1 uses `b[i]` (original), `c[i]`, `d[i]`.  
- Statement 2 uses `c[i]` and `b[i]` (original), overwrites `b[i]`.  
- Statement 3 uses `b[i]` (updated by statement 2) and `a[i+1]` (original value from before the loop iteration for `i`), and `d[i]`.  

Crucially, `a[i+1]` in statement 3 is **not** the value computed in statement 1 for `i+1` (because `i+1` hasn’t been processed yet in this iteration of the outer `i` loop), but the value from the **previous iteration of the outer loop** (or initial values for first iteration of outer loop).  

In the optimized code:  
- It stores `a[i+1]` for all `i` before any updates.  
- Then computes `a[i]` for all `i` (this overwrites `a[0..LEN_1D-2]`).  
- Then updates `b[i]` for all `i`.  
- Then computes `a[i+1]` using stored original `a[i+1]` and updated `b[i]`.  

Check carefully: In original, when computing `a[i+1]` in iteration `i`, `a[i+1]` is the original from start of current `nl` iteration, not from earlier `i` in same `nl`.  
In optimized, `a_next_original[i]` = `a[i+1]` at start of `nl`, which matches original’s requirement.  

But wait — in original, `a[i]` is computed in iteration `i`, then `a[i+1]` is computed in iteration `i`.  
In optimized, `a[i]` is computed for all `i`, then `a[i+1]` is computed for all `i`.  
This changes **order of writes to `a`** but not final values, because `a[i]` and `a[i+1]` are independent in terms of source data within same `nl` (except `a[i+1]` uses original `a[i+1]`, not updated one).  

Let’s test with a small example:  

Take `LEN_1D = 3`, `iterations = 1`, initial:  
a = [a0, a1, a2]  
b = [b0, b1, b2]  
c = [c0, c1, c2]  
d = [d0, d1, d2]  

**Original execution** (`i=0`, `i=1`):  
i=0:  
a0 = b0 + c0*d0  
b0 = c0 + b0  
a1 = b0(new) + a1(original)*d0  

i=1:  
a1 = b1 + c1*d1  ← **overwrites a1 just computed in i=0!**  
b1 = c1 + b1  
a2 = b1(new) + a2(original)*d1  

So `a1` final = b1 + c1*d1, **not** b0 + a1_original*d0.  
Thus the original code’s statement 1 for `i+1` overwrites statement 3’s result for `i`.  

**Optimized code**:  
Step 1: store a_next_original[0]=a1, a_next_original[1]=a2.  
Step 2: compute a[i] for i=0..1:  
a0 = b0 + c0*d0  
a1 = b1 + c1*d1  
Step 3: update b[i] for i=0..1:  
b0 = c0 + b0  
b1 = c1 + b1  
Step 4: compute a[i+1] for i=0..1:  
a1 = b0 + a_next_original[0]*d0  
a2 = b1 + a_next_original[1]*d1  

But step 4’s `a1` overwrites step 2’s `a1`.  

Compare final `a1` in original vs optimized:  
Original final `a1` = b1 + c1*d1 (from i=1 statement 1).  
Optimized final `a1` = b0 + a1_original*d0 (from step 4 with i=0).  

These are different unless b1 + c1*d1 = b0 + a1_original*d0, which is not generally true.  

Thus **semantics differ**.  

---

**3. Conclusion**  
The optimized code changes the meaning because it fails to account for the **overwrite of a[i+1] in the next iteration of i** in the original loop.  
The compiler feedback shows vectorization of the wrong semantics.  

Since semantics are inconsistent, we stop here.

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     ```  

Thus there’s a **true dependency** between step 3 at `i` and step 1 at `i+1`? Let’s check:

- Step 1 at `i+1` writes `a[i+1]` again (overwrites).
- So step 3’s result is immediately overwritten by next iteration’s step 1 — except for the last iteration (`i = LEN_1D-2`), step 3 writes `a[LEN_1D-1]`, which is never overwritten by step 1 (since step 1 runs only for `i` up to `LEN_1D-2`).

Unit Test analysis:

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;
     ```

UNIT TEST PASS

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

Let’s go step-by-step.  

**1. Compilation errors in optimized code**  
The optimized code compiles without errors — no syntax issues.  

**2. Semantic comparison**  
The original loop:  

```c
for (int i = 0; i < LEN_1D-1; ++i) {
    a[i] = b[i] + c[i] * d[i];
    b[i] = c[i] + b[i];
    a[i+1] = b[i] + a[i+1] * d[i];
}
```

Important dependencies:  
- `b[i]` is updated in the second statement, then used in the third statement (`a[i+1] = b[i] + ...`).  
- `a[i+1]` on the RHS of the third statement is the **original** `a[i+1]` from before the iteration starts, because `a[i+1]` was not written earlier in this iteration (except possibly in the previous iteration when `i` was smaller).  

The optimized code splits into three separate loops:  

1. First loop:  
   ```c
   a_next[i] = a[i+1];
   a[i] = b[i] + c[i] * d[i];
   ```
   This saves original `a[i+1]` and computes new `a[i]`.  

2. Second loop:  
   ```c
   b[i] = c[i] + b[i];
   ```
   This updates `b[i]` using original `b[i]` from before the outer iteration.  

3. Third loop:  
   ```c
   a[i+1] = b[i] + a_next[i] * d[i];
   ```
   This uses updated `b[i]` from second loop and original `a[i+1]` from `a_next[i]`.  

Let’s test with a small example:  

**Example 1:**  
`LEN_1D = 3`, `iterations = 1`.  
Initial:  
`a = [A0, A1, A2]`, `b = [B0, B1, B2]`, `c = [C0, C1, C2]`, `d = [D0, D1, D2]`.  

**Original execution (`i=0`):**  
`a[0] = B0 + C0*D0`  
`b[0] = C0 + B0`  
`a[1] = b[0] + A1*D0`  

(`i=1`):  
`a[1] = B1 + C1*D1`  ← **overwrites** previous `a[1]` from i=0! Wait — careful: In original code, `a[1]` is computed in iteration i=0, then recomputed in iteration i=1. Yes, because loop goes i=0..LEN_1D-2, so i=1 will execute:  
`a[1] = b[1] + c[1]*d[1]`  
`b[1] = c[1] + b[1]`  
`a[2] = b[1] + a[2]*d[1]`  

So `a[1]` is written twice: once in i=0’s third statement, once in i=1’s first statement.  

Let’s trace fully:  

**Original:**  
i=0:  
a0_new = B0 + C0*D0  
b0_new = C0 + B0  
a1_new = b0_new + A1*D0  

i=1:  
a1_new2 = B1 + C1*D1  
b1_new = C1 + B1  
a2_new = b1_new + A2*D1  

Final: a = [a0_new, a1_new2, a2_new], b = [b0_new, b1_new, B2]  

**Optimized:**  
Loop1 (i=0..1):  
a_next[0] = A1  
a_next[1] = A2  
a[0] = B0 + C0*D0  
a[1] = B1 + C1*D1  

Loop2 (i=0..1):  
b[0] = C0 + B0  
b[1] = C1 + B1  

Loop3 (i=0..1):  
a[1] = b[0] + a_next[0]*D0 = (C0+B0) + A1*D0  
a[2] = b[1] + a_next[1]*D1 = (C1+B1) + A2*D1  

Final: a = [a0_new, (C0+B0)+A1*D0, (C1+B1)+A2*D1]  

Compare:  
Original a[1] = B1 + C1*D1  
Optimized a[1] = (C0+B0) + A1*D0 → **different**.  

So semantics are **not** preserved. The optimized code’s `a[1]` after loop1 is overwritten in loop3, but original code’s `a[1]` after i=0 is overwritten in i=1’s first statement. The optimized code’s loop3 writes to `a[1]` using `b[0]`, but original’s final `a[1]` comes from i=1’s first statement using original `b[1]`, not `b[0]`.  

Thus, **FAIL** due to semantic mismatch.  

**3. Vectorization analysis**  
Not needed because semantics are wrong.  

**Conclusion:**  
The optimized code changes the meaning of the program. The main issue is that in the original, `a[i+1]` computed in iteration `i` is temporary and gets overwritten in iteration `i+1` (except for the last `i`). The optimized code’s loop3 incorrectly finalizes `a[i+1]` for all `i` without allowing the next iteration’s first statement to overwrite it.
